```typescript
  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
    models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
      .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
        const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
        if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-008](#m-008) — Use parameterized database queries (`login.ts:34`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-sql-injection-in-login)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · NoSQL \$where injection enables JavaScript execution (showProductReviews.ts:36)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-6](#tb-6) - juice-shop-api → juice-shop-db · Query construction: \`$where` JavaScript injection at `showProductReviews.ts:36` breaks the data-interpretation assumption leg of tb-6, which requires parameterized ORM methods for all queries.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker calls GET `/rest/products/1`;`sleep(2000)``/reviews`. The path parameter is interpolated into a MongoDB \`$where` expression as `{ $where: 'this.product == 1;sleep(2000)' }`.

MongoDB executes the JavaScript, triggering the `global.sleep` call that blocks Node\.js event processing for 2 seconds. With concurrent requests the event loop is starved, denying service to all users.

Arbitrary JavaScript execution in MongoDB enables event-loop DoS via sleep injection, cross-collection data extraction bypassing query filters, and potential privilege escalation through access to MongoDB collections outside the intended query scope.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`: `db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id })` where `id` is the attacker-controlled URL parameter. `global.sleep` at lines 17-25 provides a ready-made blocking primitive. Recon signal INJ-NODE-006 at line 36 corroborates the injection site.

```typescript
// routes/showProductReviews.ts:36
    // Measure how long the query takes, to check if there was a nosql dos attack
    const t0 = new Date().getTime()

    db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id }).then((reviews: Review[]) => {
      const t1 = new Date().getTime()
      challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlCommandChallenge, () => { return (t1 - t0) > 2000 })
      const user = security.authenticatedUsers.from(req)
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ● [M-009](#m-009) — Use parameterized database queries (`showProductReviews.ts:36`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-nosql-where-injection-enables-javascript-execution)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (lib/insecurity.ts:21)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker who reads the source code - via public repository, git clone, or a source-disclosure vulnerability - extracts the RSA private key literal and calls `jwt.sign({ data: { id: 1, role: 'admin' } }, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })` locally. The forged JWT is accepted by all protected API routes, granting full admin access without any credential.

Ability to forge valid JWTs for any user with any role, exposing all user account records, payment card details, order history, personal data, and admin functions - the full set of sensitive assets for this component.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:21`: `const privateKey = '[PEM PRIVATE KEY — REDACTED]\r\nMIICXAIBAAKBgQD...'` - the full 1024-bit RSA private key is a hardcoded string literal in source. The recon signal AUTHZ-007 at `lib/insecurity.ts:53` flags the signing key exposure. CRYPTO-001 signal at `lib/insecurity.ts:41` corroborates crypto weaknesses.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-010](#m-010) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-hardcoded-rsa-private-key-enables-offline-jwt-forgery)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>
#### F-011 · Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body routes/verify.ts:53

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/verify.ts:53`

**Issue:** Server code that consumes `req.body.role` / `req.body.isAdmin` / etc. without an explicit allowlist trusts the client to behave. An attacker simply adds `{"role":"admin"}` to their request to escalate.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
// routes/verify.ts:53

export const registerAdminChallenge = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.registerAdminChallenge, () => {
    return req.body && req.body.role === security.roles.admin
  })
  next()
}
```

**Fix:** ● [M-011](#m-011) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (`verify.ts:53`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-mass-assignment-privileged-field-accepted-from-request-body)

### 🟠 High (24)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · XSS via Angular Sanitizer Bypass

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Angular SPA Frontend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Instances (3):** 🟠 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`, 🟡 `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`

**Issue:** An attacker crafts a URL with a malicious HTML/script payload in the ?q= query parameter; when a victim follows the link, `filterTable()` passes the unsanitized value through `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()`, causing Angular to render it as trusted HTML in the search results view and executing the injected script in the victim's browser session. The injected script runs in the victim's same-origin context, enabling theft of the JWT from `localStorage`, session hijacking, account takeover, and further actions on behalf of the victim.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `search-result.component.ts:143` passes the raw URL query param `queryParam` directly to `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml()`, bypassing Angular's XSS sanitizer; the resulting SafeHtml value is bound to `searchValue` which is rendered in the template without further escaping.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143
      }) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
      this.dataSource.filter = queryParam.toLowerCase()
      this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line localXssChallenge xssBonusChallenge
      if (this.gridDataSourceSubscription) {
        this.gridDataSourceSubscription.unsubscribe()
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-002](#m-002) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

#### F-012 · Security-question password reset without secondary verification

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`

**Issue:** An attacker who knows a victim's email address submits POST `/rest/user/reset-password` with the correct security answer - researchable from public social media or challenge hints embedded in the application - and receives a 200 response that updates the victim's password without any email-confirmation token or MFA challenge. Account takeover for any user whose security answer is guessable or discoverable, exposing order history, saved payment cards, personal addresses, and LLM conversation context.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `routes/resetPassword.ts:10-41` accepts email + security answer and calls `user.update({ password })` with no email-confirmation token, no out-of-band verification, and no MFA step. The security answer is the only ownership proof.

```typescript
import type { Memory as MemoryConfig } from '../lib/config.schema'
import { SecurityAnswerModel } from '../models/securityAnswer'
import * as challengeUtils from '../lib/challengeUtils'
import { challenges, users } from '../data/datacache'
```

<a id="t-013"></a><a id="f-013"></a>
#### F-013 · OAuth Implicit Flow Exposes Access Token in URL Fragment

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**Issue:** `login.component.ts:148` initiates the Google OAuth flow with `response_type=token`, requesting the legacy implicit flow; Google delivers the `access_token` in the URL fragment (after the #); the token appears in browser history, is accessible to any JavaScript on the page, and may be included in `Referer` headers; an attacker who can observe the URL (via browser history access, shared session, or network interception) can steal the `access_token` and use it to call Google's userinfo endpoint to obtain the victim's email, then compute the deterministic Juice Shop password (finding 004) and log in as the victim. Google OAuth access token is exposed in the URL fragment and browser history; combined with the deterministic password derivation (finding 004), the token enables full account takeover for any OAuth-registered user whose redirect URL is observed.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:148` constructs the Google OAuth URL with `response_type=token`; the recon signal oauth-implicit-flow at this line and oauth-missing-state at the same line confirm the implicit flow without a CSRF state parameter.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148

  googleLogin () {
    this.windowRefService.nativeWindow.location.replace(`${oauthProviderUrl}?client_id=${this.clientId}&response_type=token&scope=email&redirect_uri=${this.redirectUri}`)
  }
}
```

**Fix:** Strengthen authentication: enforce a vetted JWT verifier with explicit algorithm, MFA where appropriate → ◕ [M-013](#m-013) — Harden the authentication flow (`login.component.ts:148`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

#### F-016 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - Socket\.IO Real-Time Server  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → juice-shop-realtime · Authentication: This boundary 'authentication' leg requires connections to be accepted only after verifying a valid JWT; line 23 accepts all connections with no token check.

**Instances (3):** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:23`, `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:34`, `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:40`

**Issue:** An anonymous internet client opens a WebSocket upgrade to `ws://localhost:3000/socket.io`, which the server accepts without verifying any JWT or session token at the connection handler. The client immediately receives all queued challenge-solved notifications and can emit any named event as if it were an authenticated user.

Any unauthenticated internet client assumes an equal standing to authenticated users within the Socket\.IO namespace, receiving event data and triggering server-side state changes without holding a valid identity.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `io.on('connection', (socket: any) => { ... })` at line 23 fires for every inbound connection with no preceding authentication middleware; the architecture context explicitly flags JWT verification as 'assumed but not confirmed from `registerWebsocketEvents.ts` source'.

```typescript

  io.on('connection', (socket: any) => {
    if (firstConnectedSocket === null) {
      socket.emit('server started')
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:23`)
