<a id="m-009"></a>
#### M-009 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — NoSQL \$where injection enables JavaScript execution (`showProductReviews.ts:36`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. Replace `db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id })` at `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36` with `db.reviewsCollection.find({ product: Number(id) })` - typed numeric comparison without JavaScript execution.
2. Remove the `global.sleep` blocking function at `routes/showProductReviews.ts:17-25`; `replace` with `performance.now()` for non-blocking timing measurement.
3. Add a test that submits `1;sleep(2000)` as the product id and asserts the response completes in under 100ms.

_Example implementation in `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Reject `$where` and operator keys from user-controlled input.
function safeQuery(filter) {
  for (const k of Object.keys(filter)) {
    if (k.startsWith('$')) throw new Error('operator not allowed')
  }
  return collection.find(filter)
}
```

**Verification:** Send GET `/rest/products/1`;`sleep(2000)``/reviews` and assert the response arrives in under 200ms; verify the query log shows no \`$where` operator.

**Reference:** [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic

---

<a id="m-010"></a>
#### M-010 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the hardcoded private key string at `lib/insecurity.ts:21` and `replace` with `process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` or a secret manager read at startup; throw on missing value to prevent silent fallback.
2. Rotate the key: generate a new 4096-bit RSA key pair, deploy the private key via the secret store, and store only the public key in `encryptionkeys/jwt.pub`.
3. Revoke all existing tokens by changing the key; add a startup assertion that the loaded private key is at least 2048 bits.
4. Add a secret-scanning CI step (e.g., trufflehog or git-secrets) that fails the pipeline if a private key pattern appears in any committed file.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:21`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** Run `grep -r 'BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY' .` in the repository root and assert zero matches; start the application without `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` set and assert it exits with a non-zero code.

**Reference:** [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

---

<a id="m-011"></a>
#### M-011 — Allowlist client-controlled fields

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body (`verify.ts:53`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:53`

**How:** Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy)…

1. Apply an allowlist filter (Joi/Zod/yup schema, `_.pick`, or explicit field copy) before passing the body to any model, and strip privilege fields before persistence.
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Example implementation in `routes/verify.ts:53`: it applies **Allowlist client-controlled fields**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Whitelist mutable fields server-side; never trust the body.
const ALLOWED = ['email', 'username'] as const
const patch = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(req.body).filter(([k]) => ALLOWED.includes(k as any))
)
await user.update(patch)
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-003 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/verify.ts:53`, and the regression test passes.

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-002"></a>
#### M-002 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — XSS via Angular Sanitizer Bypass (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`

**How:**

1. Replace `this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)` at line 143 with `this.searchValue = queryParam` and change the template binding from [`innerHTML`] or [`outerHTML`] to text interpolation {{ `searchValue` }} to let Angular's default escaping handle the value.
2. Write an e2e test that navigates to `/search?q`=`<script>``alert(1)``</script>` and asserts that no alert dialog fires and the raw tag appears as escaped text.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`: it applies **Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Never call bypassSecurityTrust*; let Angular sanitize.
// template:  <div [innerHTML]="product.description"></div>
// component: no DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(...)
this.product.description = raw  // bound directly; Angular escapes
```

**Verification:** Navigate to /#`/search?q`=`<img+src=x+onerror=alert(1)>` and verify that no alert fires and the browser's DOM shows the string as escaped text, not an img element.

**Reference:** [angular.dev: Security](https://angular.dev/guide/security#preventing-cross-site-scripting-xss)

<a id="m-012"></a>
#### M-012 — Replace security-question reset with email-token flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Security-question password reset without secondary verification (`resetPassword.ts:10`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`

**How:**

1. On receipt of a reset request, generate a short-lived signed token, store its hash in the Users table with an expiry, and email it to the address on file instead of accepting a security answer.
2. Remove SecurityAnswerModel lookup at `routes/resetPassword.ts:35` and `replace` with token comparison.
3. Add a test that posts a valid security answer and asserts 403, and a second test that uses a valid email token and asserts 200.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/reset-password` with a known-correct security answer; assert status 403 and the password in the database is unchanged.

**Reference:** OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet - Forgot Password section

<a id="m-013"></a>
#### M-013 — Harden the authentication flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — OAuth Implicit Flow Exposes Access Token in URL Fragment (`login.component.ts:148`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Change the Google OAuth redirect at `login.component.ts:148` from `response_type=token` to `response_type=code` and add `code_challenge` and `code_challenge_method=S256` PKCE parameters.
2. Implement a backend token exchange endpoint that accepts the authorization code, verifies the PKCE `code_verifier`, and exchanges the code for tokens at Google's token endpoint.
3. Generate and validate a cryptographically random state parameter per authorization request to prevent CSRF on the OAuth callback.

**Verification:** Initiate OAuth login and confirm the redirect URL contains `response_type=code` (not token); confirm the callback URL contains code= not `access_token`= in the fragment.

**Reference:** [oauth.net: Pkce](https://oauth.net/2/pkce/)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

2. Add a backend endpoint POST `/rest/oauth/google` that accepts the Google ID token, verifies it with Google's JWKS, and issues a Juice Shop session JWT without requiring a password.
3. Ensure existing OAuth accounts are migrated to the new credential-less flow before deployment.

<a id="m-015"></a>
#### M-015 — Harden the authentication flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Predictable OAuth password derivation (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

2. Remove the `btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))` derivation at `oauth.component.ts:30` and the corresponding login call with the same derivation at :46; instead, issue a session directly from the server's OAuth callback handler after verifying the IdP token.
3. Add an integration test that registers a user via OAuth and then attempts to log in with the derived-password formula, asserting HTTP 401.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/login` with {email: 'test@`example.com`', password: `btoa('moc.elpmaxe@tset')`} must return HTTP 401; server logs must show authentication failure.

<a id="m-021"></a>
#### M-021 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-022](#f-022) — Hardcoded HMAC Secret Enables Arbitrary Password Reset (`insecurity.ts:42`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:42`

**How:**

1. Replace the literal 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj' at `lib/insecurity.ts:42` with `process.env.SECURITY_ANSWER_HMAC_KEY` and require the environment variable to be set at startup.
2. Rehash all existing security answers with the new secret so that precomputed digests using the old secret are invalid.
3. Add a test asserting that `hmac()` with an empty or missing environment variable throws rather than silently using a default.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:42`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** After deployment with a rotated secret, confirm that a precomputed HMAC using 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj' against a known answer is rejected by POST `/rest/user/reset-password`.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — MD5 Password Hash Without Salt Enables Offline Recovery (`insecurity.ts:41`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace `hash()` at `lib/insecurity.ts:41` with `bcrypt.hash(data, 12)` from the bcrypt npm package; update all callers in `routes/login.ts` and `routes/2fa.ts` to use `bcrypt.compare()` for verification.
2. Run a migration that rehashes all stored passwords: on next login, verify the old `MD5` hash, then store the bcrypt hash; force password reset for all users after a transition deadline.
3. Add a test confirming that identical passwords produce different stored hashes (salt randomness) and that the hash computation takes at least 100ms on the build machine.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:41`: it applies **Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Replace md5/sha1 password hashes with bcrypt or argon2.
import bcrypt from 'bcrypt'
const hash = await bcrypt.hash(plaintext, 12)
const ok = await bcrypt.compare(plaintext, storedHash)
```

**Verification:** Extract a password hash from the Users table; verify that `echo -n 'password' | md5sum` does NOT match the stored value; verify that `bcrypt.compare('password', storedHash)` returns true.

**Reference:** [CWE-916: Use of Password Hash with Insufficient Computational Effort](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

<a id="m-026"></a>
#### M-026 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-027](#f-027) — JWT bearer token stored in localStorage (`request.interceptor.ts:13`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:13`

**How:**

1. Change the authentication flow to return the JWT exclusively via a `Set-Cookie` header with HttpOnly, Secure, and `SameSite=Strict` attributes, making it inaccessible to JavaScript.
2. Remove the `localStorage.setItem('token')` calls at `login.component.ts:101` and `oauth.component.ts:51`; update `request.interceptor.ts` to remove the `Authorization` header injection since the browser will attach the cookie automatically.
3. Verify that `document.cookie` does not expose the session token and that JavaScript cannot read it; confirm requests still authenticate by inspecting network traffic in browser devtools.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/Services/request.interceptor.ts:13`: it applies **Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Move the JWT out of localStorage into an httpOnly cookie.
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'lax', maxAge: 3600_000
})
```

**Verification:** After login, run `localStorage.getItem('token')` in the browser console and assert it returns null; confirm authenticated API calls succeed via the HttpOnly cookie.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Session Management Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Session_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html#cookies)

```typescript
app.use('/metrics', requireRole('admin'), promBundle())
```

<a id="m-028"></a>
#### M-028 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-029](#f-029) — NoSQL \$where JS injection returns all orders (`trackOrder.ts:18`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**How:**

1. At `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`, `replace` `db.ordersCollection.find({ $where: \`this.`orderId` === '\${id}'\` })` with `db.ordersCollection.find({ orderId: id })` to use MarsDB's built-in field comparison without JavaScript evaluation.
2. Ensure `id` is still sanitized (alphanumeric + hyphen only) regardless of challenge state, removing the conditional sanitization branch.
3. Add a test that sends a tracking request with id = "'; return true; //" and asserts exactly zero or one order is returned (matching the crafted ID), not all orders.

_Example implementation in `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Reject `$where` and operator keys from user-controlled input.
function safeQuery(filter) {
  for (const k of Object.keys(filter)) {
    if (k.startsWith('$')) throw new Error('operator not allowed')
  }
  return collection.find(filter)
}
```

**Verification:** GET `/rest/track-order/``' OR '1'='1` - assert the response contains only orders with a matching `orderId`, not all orders in the collection.

**Reference:** [OWASP NoSQL Injection](https://owasp.org/www-project-web-security-testing-guide/latest/4-Web_Application_Security_Testing/07-Input_Validation_Testing/05.6-Testing_for_NoSQL_Injection)

Inject the actual mnemonic through Docker environment, Kubernetes secret, or local .env file, not source code.

<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Apply least-privilege filesystem access

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Missing GITHUB_TOKEN permissions restriction (`ci.yml:1`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**How:**

1. Add `permissions: read-all` at the top level of `ci.yml` (after the `env:` block) as a deny-all default.
2. Grant only the minimum needed per job: add `permissions: { contents: write, packages: write }` to the docker job; `permissions: { contents: read }` to test jobs; `statuses: write` or `checks: write` to coverage jobs as needed.
3. Apply the same pattern to `image_actions.yml`, which also lacks a permissions block.
4. Verify with StepSecurity's harden-runner action or `actionlint` that no job inherits write-all `GITHUB_TOKEN` after the change.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Centralize access decisions in a single middleware.
function requireRole(role: 'admin' | 'user') {
  return (req, res, next) =>
    req.user?.role === role ? next() : res.status(403).end()
}
```

**Verification:** Run `python3 -c "import yaml,sys; w=yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml')); sys.exit(0 if 'permissions' in w else 1)"` - must exit 0.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Automatic Token Authentication](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-guides/automatic-token-authentication#modifying-the-permissions-for-the-github_token)

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-034](#f-034) — Code execution via eval on stored username (`userProfile.ts:61`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Remove the entire `if (username?.match(/#{(.*)}/) !== null && utils.isChallengeEnabled(challenges.usernameXssChallenge))` block at `routes/userProfile.ts:54-61` and the `eval(code)` call.
2. Replace with HTML entity encoding of the raw username before template substitution using the `entities` object already imported at `userProfile.ts:18`.
3. Add a test that sets username to `#{process.mainModule.require('os').platform()}` and visits `/profile`, asserting the username is rendered as literal text and no OS command output appears in the response.

_Example implementation in `routes/userProfile.ts:61`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Replace `eval()` / sandbox with a strict whitelist parser.
import { evaluate } from 'mathjs'
const allowed = /^[\d+\-*/().\s]+$/
if (!allowed.test(expr)) throw new Error('invalid expression')
return evaluate(expr)
```

**Verification:** Set username to `#{process.mainModule.require('os').platform()}` via the profile update API, then GET `/profile`; assert the response body contains the literal string `#{process` and does not contain `linux` or `darwin` as a substituted value.

**Reference:** [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Improper Control of Generation of Code

<a id="m-034"></a>
#### M-034 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-035](#f-035) — Missing ownership check enables cross-user bulk review update (`updateProductReviews.ts:18`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing `Authorization`

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Before calling `reviewsCollection.update()`, fetch the target review and compare `review.author` against `user.data.email`; return HTTP 403 if they differ.
2. Change the update call at `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:17-21` to remove { `multi: true` } so only a single document can be modified per authenticated request.
3. Add a test that authenticates as alice, attempts to update a review authored by bob, and asserts HTTP 403 is returned with the review unchanged.

_Example implementation in `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Add server-side role check on every admin route.
router.use('/admin/*', (req, res, next) => {
  if (req.user?.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).end()
  next()
})
```

**Verification:** Authenticate as alice@`example.com`, PATCH `/rest/reviews` with `body.id` pointing to a review authored by bob@`example.com`; assert HTTP 403 and that bob's review message is unchanged in the database.

**Reference:** [CWE-862: Missing Authorization](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html)

<a id="m-004"></a>
#### M-004 — Apply least-privilege filesystem access

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-004](#f-004) — No DB-level principal isolation for in-process SQLite (`user.ts:20`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** High · **File:** `models/user.ts:20`

**How:**

1. Create a read-only Sequelize connection (SQLite URI with `mode=ro`) for components that require only SELECT access; wire it into the Sequelize model init for those components.
2. Restrict write connections to the minimal set of models that require INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE.
3. Document the intended access matrix in `models/index.ts` so code review can catch escalation on future model additions.
4. Add a CI check that asserts no write Sequelize method (create, update, destroy, upsert) is called from read-only component modules.

_Example implementation in `models/user.ts:20`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Centralize access decisions in a single middleware.
function requireRole(role: 'admin' | 'user') {
  return (req, res, next) =>
    req.user?.role === role ? next() : res.status(403).end()
}
```

**Verification:** Attempting a `UserModel.update()` call from a module initialised with the read-only connection throws a SQLite READONLY error.

**Reference:** [CWE-284: Improper Access Control](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html)

<a id="m-037"></a>
#### M-037 — Harden the authentication flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-038](#f-038) — Wallet identity accepted on HTTP body claim alone (`nftMint.ts:41`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/nftMint.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Issue a server-signed nonce tied to the calling user's JWT session when a registration or verify request arrives. Require the client to sign this nonce with their MetaMask wallet via eth_personalSign. Recover the signer address from the signature and verify it matches `walletAddress` before touching `addressesMinted`.
2. Add an integration test that submits a `walletAddress` of a known minted address without a matching signature and asserts HTTP 401 is returned; a valid signature returns HTTP 200 and the address is removed.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/nft/mint` with `walletAddress` of a known minted address but no signature returns HTTP 401 and `addressesMinted` is unchanged; posting with a valid eth_personalSign over the server nonce returns HTTP 200.

**Reference:** [eips.ethereum.org: Eip 4361](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-4361)

<a id="m-038"></a>
#### M-038 — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-039](#f-039) — Mutable container base image tag without digest (`Dockerfile:1`) (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) - Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:**

1. Replace `FROM node:24` with `FROM node:24@sha256:<current-digest>` and `FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13` with its digest-pinned equivalent.
2. Replace `bkimminich/juice-shop:latest` in `docker-compose.test.yml` line 7 with a pinned digest or version tag.
3. Add Renovate or Dependabot `docker` manager configuration to receive automated PRs when upstream digests change.

_Example implementation in `Dockerfile:1`: it applies **Pin the container base image to an immutable digest**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```bash
# Pin and audit dependencies; fail CI on known vulns.
npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high
# Upgrade unmaintained packages in package.json, then:
npm install && npm test
```

**Verification:** Run `grep -n '^FROM' Dockerfile` - every line must match the pattern `FROM <image>@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}`.

**Reference:** [docs.docker.com: Builder](https://docs.docker.com/engine/reference/builder/#from)

#### M-040 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - `Authorization` Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/nftMint.ts:43`

**How:**

2. Write a test that POSTs a known minted address without a valid signature and confirms the address is still present in `addressesMinted` after the request.

_Example implementation in `routes/nftMint.ts:43`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

<a id="m-042"></a>
#### M-042 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

**How:**

1. Remove `localStorage.setItem('token', ...)` at `login.component.ts:101` and lines 120, 124, 135; issue the JWT as an HttpOnly `SameSite=Strict` cookie from the server-side login response.
2. Update `app.guard.ts:18` to infer login state from a non-sensitive indicator (e.g., a non-HttpOnly boolean cookie or a /me API response) rather than reading the token directly from `localStorage`.
3. Add a test confirming that `document.cookie` after login does not include the token value and that `localStorage` does not contain the token.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`: it applies **Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Move the JWT out of localStorage into an httpOnly cookie.
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'lax', maxAge: 3600_000
})
```

<a id="m-044"></a>
#### M-044 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:32`

2. Return 429 Too Many Requests with a Retry-After header when the limit is exceeded.
3. Add a test that submits 11 login attempts from the same IP within one minute and asserts that the 11th request returns 429.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:32`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Per-IP + per-account rate limiting on auth endpoints.
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit'
app.use('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60_000, max: 5, standardHeaders: true }))
```

<a id="m-048"></a>
#### M-048 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-049](#f-049) — Prompt injection bypasses coupon discount cap via unconstrained schema (`chat.ts:179`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:179`

**How:**

1. Change the Zod schema at `routes/chat.ts:179` from `z.number()` to `z.number().min(1).max(10)` so the AI SDK rejects any LLM-supplied discount outside the permitted range before the execute function is called.
2. Add a guard at the start of the execute function: `if (discount > 10) return { error: 'Discount exceeds policy maximum' }` as a defense-in-depth fallback.
3. Add an integration test that directly calls the `generateCoupon` tool with `discount=80` and asserts it returns an error, not a coupon code.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:179`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Replace `eval()` / sandbox with a strict whitelist parser.
import { evaluate } from 'mathjs'
const allowed = /^[\d+\-*/().\s]+$/
if (!allowed.test(expr)) throw new Error('invalid expression')
return evaluate(expr)
```

**Verification:** Inject a chat message requesting an 80% discount coupon; assert the SSE stream returns an error event and no `couponCode` value appears in the response.

**Reference:** OWASP LLM01:2025 - Prompt Injection

---

<a id="m-049"></a>
#### M-049 — Enforce authorization on the server

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-050](#f-050) — Client-Side-Only Admin Role Check in AdminGuard (`app.guard.ts:54`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Replace the `tokenDecode()` call in `AdminGuard.canActivate()` with an HTTP call to a server-side endpoint (e.g., GET `/rest/me`) that verifies the JWT signature and returns the user role; allow navigation only if the server confirms `role=admin`.
2. Remove the use of `jwtDecode` for authorization decisions in `app.guard.ts`; retain it only for non-security-sensitive display purposes such as showing the user's email in the UI.
3. Add an e2e test that sets `localStorage.token` to an unsigned JWT with `role=admin` and asserts that navigation to `/administration` redirects to the 403 page.

**Verification:** Set `localStorage.token` in the browser console to a base64-encoded JWT with `data.role`=`'admin'` but no valid signature; navigate to `/administration` and assert the page redirects to 403.

**Reference:** [CWE-602: Client-Side Enforcement of Server-Side Security](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

---

<a id="m-050"></a>
#### M-050 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-051](#f-051) — Challenge solve granted without verifying caller performed the on-chain exploit (`web3Wallet.ts:15`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing `Authorization`

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:15`

**How:**

1. Replace the `walletsConnected` Set with a Map<string, string> keyed on `walletAddress` and valued on the JWT subject (`req.user.email` or sub). When ContractExploited fires at line 26, resolve the challenge for `walletsConnected.get(exploiter)` rather than for any caller.
2. Require EIP-4361 wallet-ownership proof at registration time (line 15-16) so the JWT subject is cryptographically bound to the wallet address before it is added to the Map.
3. Write a test: register address A as user U1, trigger ContractExploited for address A, confirm `challenges.web3WalletChallenge` is solved only for U1's session and not for a different user session that also registered A.

_Example implementation in `routes/web3Wallet.ts:15`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Add server-side role check on every admin route.
router.use('/admin/*', (req, res, next) => {
  if (req.user?.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).end()
  next()
})
```

**Verification:** Register address A as user U1; simulate a ContractExploited event for A; confirm that only U1's challenge status shows solved and that user U2 (who also registered A) does not receive credit.

**Reference:** [eips.ethereum.org: Eip 4361](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-4361)

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

This is a code-derived threat model at implementation level: it is built from source, configuration and git history, models the system as built rather than as designed, and does not `replace` a design-time review.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys that leave the code.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Vektor Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege user (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid user session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level user (JWT with admin role or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin role but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external data fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### n/a

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
